\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}}:=\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}}\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{b}}.

Here is a short glossary for the most important spaces in applications:

•

\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}
-
\delta
-Hölder continuous functions,

•

\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}
-
l
-times continuously differentiable functions such that their
l
-th derivative is
\delta
-Hölder continuous,

•

\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{b}
- bounded
l
-times continuously differentiable functions such that all their derivatives are bounded, and their
l
-th derivative is
\delta
-Hölder continuous

•

\operatorname{Lip}
- Lipschitz continuous functions,

•

\operatorname{Lip}^{l}
-
l-1
-times continuously differentiable functions such that they and all their derivatives are Lipschitz continuous,

•

\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}
-
l
-times continuously differentiable functions such that they and their derivatives up to order
k-1
are Lipschitz continuous, and such that their
l
-th derivative is
\delta
-Hölder continuous,

•

\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}
(
=\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}
) - continuous functions with at most polynomial growth of order
\kappa
,

•

\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}
(
=\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}
) -
l
-times continuously differentiable functions such that they and all their derivatives have at most polynomial growth of order
\kappa
.

A lowercase
b
(as in
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{b}
) means functions and all their derivatives (which are implied to exist) are bounded.

The following statement is a generalization of the fact that a function
f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
on a convex domain is Lipschitz if and only if its derivative is bounded.

Lemma 7
.

Let
f:U\to V\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
and
U
convex.
Then
f\in\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}
if and only if
\nabla f\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}
.
Further,

\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\|\nabla f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}},\text{ uniformly over }f\in\mathcal{C}^{1}\cap\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}.

Proof 4.2
.

Suppose
f\in\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Then

\|f(x+hv)-f(x)\|\leq\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x,x+hv)|h||v|,

and

\mathcal{V}(x,x+hv)\lesssim\mathcal{V}(x)\vee(\mathcal{V}(x)+\mathcal{V}(hv))\lesssim\mathcal{V}(x),

uniformly over
x\in U
and
|hv|\leq 1
.
Hence,

\|Df(x)(v)\|\lesssim\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x),\text{ uniformly over }x\in U,|v|\leq 1.

In particular,

\|\partial_{j}f(x)\|\lesssim\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x)\text{ uniformly over }x\in U.

Conversely, suppose
\nabla f\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Then, by Hadarmard’s lemma
(see
21
, Theorem 3.2.2.)

\displaystyle\|f(x)-f(y)\|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|Df(x+t(y-x))(y-x)\|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|\langle\nabla f(x+t(y-x)),y-x\rangle\|\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|\nabla f(x+t(y-x))\||y-x|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\|\nabla f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(|x+t(y-x)|)|y-x|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x,y)|x-y|,

uniformly over
x,y\in U
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\delta\in(0,1]
. On a convex domain
U
we have

(a)

\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}=\{f\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}:\nabla f\in\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{\mathcal{V}}\}

with

\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\|\nabla f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},\text{ uniformly over }f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}.

(b)

\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}=\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}\cap\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}=\{f\in\mathcal{C}^{l}:\nabla^{k}f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}},k=0,\dots,l\}
, with

\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\max_{|\alpha|\leq l}\|\partial^{\alpha}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},\text{ uniformly over }f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}.

(c)

\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}=\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{b}
with

\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}}\asymp\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{b}},\text{ uniformly over }f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}.

Proof 4.3
.

(a)

By Lemma
7

\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\max_{k<l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\max_{k<l}\|\nabla^{k+1}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\|\nabla f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},

uniformly over
f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

(b)

By Lemma
7

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\asymp\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\max_{k<l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}.

For the second representation, note that

\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\lesssim\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},

and, using the fact that
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\lesssim\|\cdot\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}
,

\displaystyle\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}=

\displaystyle\max_{k\leq l}(\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max_{k\leq l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\max_{k<l}\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|\nabla^{l}f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\mathcal{V}}}\vee\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},

uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
.

(c)

Using (b), we have

\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}}=\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}}\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{b}}\asymp\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{b}},\text{ uniformly over }f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}_{b}.

Lemma 9
.

Let
F
be a Hölder-type graded Fréchet algebra,
\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W}
be weight functions,
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
p,d\in\mathbb{N}
,
e\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
convex, and
\delta,\gamma\in[0,1]
. Then:

(a)

If
WV\leq\mathcal{W}
and
\delta\leq\gamma
, then
\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}}
with
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}}}\lesssim\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}}
.

(b)

\|cf+g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\leq|c|\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}
,
f,g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}(U,F^{\Pi e}),c\in\mathbb{R}
.

(c)

\|\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\Pi\mathcal{V}},p}\lesssim\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}
, uniformly over
f_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}}(U,F^{\Pi e^{i}}),i=1,\dots,n
.

Here,
n\in\mathbb{N},q_{1},\dots,q_{n}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{-1}=1,\Pi\mathcal{V}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{V}^{i}
for weight functions
\mathcal{V}^{1},\dots,\mathcal{V}^{n}
, and
e^{1},\dots e^{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

(d)

\|\langle f,g\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}\mathcal{W}},p}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}},pq}\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}},pr}
, uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}(U,F^{\Pi e})
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}}(U,F^{\Pi e})
. Here,
q,r\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
with
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
.

(e)

\|g\circ f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{l}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma\vee l})
, uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}(U,V)
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}(V,F^{\Pi e})
.

Note that by considering polynomial weights,
F=\mathbb{R}
and
\delta=\gamma=0
, with the help of Lemma
6
, we can conclude Lemma
2.1
(which we have already used before).

Proof 4.4

(Proof of Lemma
9
)
.

In the following, we use Lemma
8
(b) whenever needed.

(a)

Given
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}
we have

\|f(x)\|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x)\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{W}(x),\quad x\in U.

Further, for
x,y\in U
with
|x-y|\leq 1
we have

\|f(x)-f(y)\|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x,y)|x-y|^{\gamma}\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{W}(x,y)|x-y|^{\delta}.

In the case
|x-y|>1
we instead estimate

\|f(x)-f(y)\|\leq\|f(x)\|+\|f(y)\|\leq 2\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{W}(x,y)|x-y|^{\delta}.

Hence,
\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq 2\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}}
.
For general
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}
we have
\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq 2\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}
for all
k<l
and
\|\nabla^{l}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{W}}}\leq 2\|\nabla^{l}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}}}
.

(b)

Given
f,g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}},c\in\mathbb{R}
and
x,y\in U
we have

\|cf(x)+g(x)\|\leq|c|\|f(x)\|+\|g(x)\|\leq(|c|\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}})\mathcal{V}(x),

and

\displaystyle\|cf(x)+g(x)-cf(y)-g(y)\|\leq

\displaystyle|c|\|f(x)-f(y)\|+\|g(x)-g(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(|c|\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})\mathcal{V}(x,y)|x-y|^{\delta}

Thus, for
f,g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
we have
\|\nabla^{k}(cf+g)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\leq|c|\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}+\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}},k\leq l
.

(c)

In the case
l=0
, we have

\|\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(x)\|_{p}\lesssim\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}(x)\|_{pq_{i}}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{V}^{i}(x)\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}\Pi\mathcal{V}(x),

uniformly over
f_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}}(U,F^{\Pi e^{i}}),i=1,\dots,n
and
x\in U
.
Further, we have

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(x)-\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(y)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{i-1}f_{j}(x)\right)\otimes(f_{i}(x)-f_{i}(y))\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{j=i+1}^{n}f_{j}(y)\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(x)-\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(y)\|_{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}(\mathcal{V}^{j}(x)\|f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}^{j}},pq_{j}})(\mathcal{V}^{i}(x,y)\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}|x-y|^{\delta})\prod_{j={i+1}}^{n}(\mathcal{V}^{j}(y)\|f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}^{j}},pq_{j}})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\Pi\mathcal{V}(x,y)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}|x-y|^{\delta},

uniformly over
f_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}}(U,F^{\Pi e^{i}}),i=1,\dots,n
and
x,y\in U
.
For general
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
recall the Leibniz formula (Proposition
5
(b))

\partial^{\alpha}\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}\right)=\sum_{\beta_{1}+\dots+\beta_{m}=\alpha}\frac{\alpha!}{\beta_{1}!\cdots\beta_{n}!}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\beta_{i}}f_{i}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\Pi\mathcal{V}},p}\leq\max_{|\alpha|=k}\|\partial^{\alpha}(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}f_{i})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\Pi\mathcal{V}},p}\lesssim\max_{|\alpha|=k}\sum_{\Sigma\beta=\alpha}\|\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\beta_{i}}f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\Pi\mathcal{V}},p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{|\alpha|=k}\sum_{\Sigma\beta=\alpha}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|\partial_{\beta_{i}}f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}}\lesssim\prod_{i=1}^{n}\|f_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}},pq_{i}},

uniformly over
k\leq l
and
f_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{i}}(U,F^{\Pi e^{i}}),i=1,\dots,n
.

(d)

Analogous to (c) for
n=2
.

(e)

Note that
\|f(x)\|_{p}=|f(x)|,x\in U,p\geq 1
. Given
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}
, we have

\displaystyle\|g(f(x))\|\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(f(x))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}})(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V})(x),

uniformly over
x\in U
, and

\displaystyle\|g(f(x))-g(f(y))\|\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\|f(x)-f(y)\|^{\gamma}\mathcal{W}(f(x),f(y))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma}|x-y|^{\delta\gamma}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(x),\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}(y))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\|f\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma}|x-y|^{\delta\gamma}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}})(\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V})(x,y),

uniformly over
x,y\in U
. Thus,

\|g\circ f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}}})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma}),

(16)

uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}
. Now, consider
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}
. Let
\alpha
be a multi-index with
|\alpha|\leq l
. Recall Faa di Bruno’s formula (Proposition
5
(c)):

\partial^{\alpha}(g\circ f)=\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha|}\sum_{\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{S}^{\alpha}_{k}}\frac{\alpha!}{\mathcal{B}!}\langle\nabla^{k}g\circ f,\partial^{\mathcal{B}}f\rangle.

Given a partition
\mathcal{B}
of
\alpha
into
k
multi-indices, we have using (a), (b) and (c)

\|\partial^{\mathcal{B}}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{l}}}\lesssim\|\partial^{\mathcal{B}}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}^{k}}}\lesssim\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\|\partial^{\beta}f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{k}\leq 1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{l},

uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
. Further, by using Inequality (
16
)

\|\nabla^{k}g\circ f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V}}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma}),

uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}
.
Thus, using (d) with
q=\infty,r=1
,

\displaystyle\|\langle\nabla^{k}g\circ f,\partial^{\mathcal{B}}f\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}\circ\mathcal{V}\cdot\mathcal{V}^{l}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{l})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}}\mathcal{W}(\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}})(1\vee\|f\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}}^{\gamma\vee l}),

uniformly over
f\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{W}}
. Using (b), the desired result follows.

4.2
Regularity of random fields

In the previous subsection, we already encountered various regularity properties that also apply to random fields. Here, we provide additional properties specific to random fields.

Fix a complete probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{\Omega},\mathbb{P})
. We consider functions
X:U\to L^{\infty-}(\Omega,V)
, where
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m},V\subseteq B
and
B
is a Banach space.

Lemma 10
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
X:U\to L^{\infty-}(\Omega,V)\in\mathcal{C}^{l}
. Then
\mathbb{E}X\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(U,V)
with

\partial^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}X=\mathbb{E}[\partial^{\alpha}X],\quad|\alpha|\leq l.

Proof 4.5
.

Let
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. Then, for all
x\in U
,

\frac{1}{h}(X(x+e_{j}h)-(X(x)))\to\partial_{j}X(x)\text{ in }L^{1}.

In particular,

\frac{1}{h}\mathbb{E}[X(x+e_{j}h)-(X(x))]\to\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}X(x)].

Thus,
\mathbb{E}X\in\mathcal{C}^{1}(U,V)
and
\partial_{j}\mathbb{E}X=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{j}X]
. By induction we get the desired property for all
|\alpha|\leq l
.

Lemma 11
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\delta\in[0,1]
,
\mathcal{V}
be a weight function and
X:U\to L^{\infty-}(\Omega,V)\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta,l}_{\mathcal{V}}
.
Then
\mathbb{E}X\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta,l}_{\mathcal{V}}(U,V)
with

\|\mathbb{E}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta,l}_{\mathcal{V}}}\leq\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta,l}_{\mathcal{V}},1}.

Proof 4.6
.

We have

\|\mathbb{E}X(x)\|_{B}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|X(x)\|_{B}]\leq\|\|X(x)\|_{B}\|_{1}\leq\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta,l}_{\mathcal{V}},1}\mathcal{V}(x),\quad x\in U,

and similarly

\|\mathbb{E}X(x)-\mathbb{E}X(y)\|_{B}\leq\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}},1}\mathcal{V}(x,y)|x-y|^{\delta},\quad x,y\in U.

By replacing
X
with
\partial^{\alpha}X
for all
|\alpha|\leq l
and using Lemma
10
, the desired result follows.

Let
e\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
T>0
.
Next, we consider random variables valued in the space of a.s. bounded paths, that is the space
B=(L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
for
t\in[0,T]
, and a filtration
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{\Omega},\mathbb{P})
satisfying the usual condition.

The norm on the
L^{p}
-space

L^{p}_{*{t}}:=L^{p}_{*{t}}(\Omega):=L^{p}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}):=\mathcal{L}_{p}(\Omega,L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))/\sim,

where
\sim
is equality almost surely, is given by

\|X\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}:=\|X:\Omega\to L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e})\|_{p}=\mathbb{E}\left[\esssup_{s\in[0,t]}|X_{s}|^{p}\right]^{1/p},

for all
p\geq 1
and
t\in[0,T]
.
In this case, almost surely equality is equivalent to indistinguishability of stochastic processes. We identify each element of
L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e})
with a measurable representative
f
such that
\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|f_{s}|=\esssup_{s\in[0,t]}|f_{s}|
. Hence, we can write
\sup
instead of
\esssup
in the following. Further, we write
L^{p}_{*}:=L^{p}_{*{T}}
,
As usual, we consider the space of random variables with finite moments

L^{\infty-}_{*}:=L^{\infty-}_{*}(\Omega):=L^{\infty-}_{*}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e})=\bigcap_{p\in\mathbb{N}}L^{p}_{*}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e})

as a Fréchet algebra with the grading
(\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}_{*}})_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
.
To make some estimates work for all parameter values we define
\|X\|_{0}=0
and
\|X\|_{L^{0}_{*{t}}}=0
, etc.

Let
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random field and
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}
be jointly measurable. Define

g(X)(\omega,t,x)=g_{t}(X_{t}(\omega,x)),\quad\omega\in\Omega,t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then
g(X):\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}
is a random field as well.

Lemma 12
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1],\lambda\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}=1+|\cdot|^{\lambda},X,Y:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be random fields and
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}
be jointly measurable.
Then

(a)

If
g\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
, then

\|g(X)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*{t}}}),\quad p\geq 1,t\in[0,T].

(b)

If
g\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
and
q,r\in[0,\infty]
with
1/q+1/q=1
, then

\|g(X)-g(Y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}(\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{pq\lambda}_{*{t}}})+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Y\|_{L^{pq\lambda}_{*{t}}}))\|X-Y\|_{L^{pr}_{*{t}}}^{\delta},\quad p\geq 1,t\in[0,T].

Proof 4.7
.

Firstly, note that

\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|x_{s}|)=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(|x_{s}|),\quad t\in[0,T],x\in L^{\infty}([0,t],\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Moreover, given
p\geq 1
and
Z\in L^{p\lambda}

\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(Z)\|_{p}\leq\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Z\|_{p\lambda}),

and so

\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq V_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*{t}}}),\quad t\in[0,T].

Further, given
Z,Z^{\prime}\in L^{p\lambda}
we have

\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(Z,Z^{\prime})\|_{p}\leq\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(Z)+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(Z^{\prime})\|_{p}\leq\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Z\|_{p\lambda})+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Z^{\prime}\|_{p\lambda}),

and so

\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X,Y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*{t}}})+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Y\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*{t}}}),\quad t\in[0,T].

(a)

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|g_{s}(X_{s})|\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|g_{r}(X_{s})|\leq\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X_{s}).

Hence, by applying
\|\cdot\|_{p}
,

\|g(X)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*{t}}}),\quad p\geq 1.

(b)

In this case, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|g_{s}(X_{s})-g_{s}(Y_{s})|\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\sup_{r\in[0,T]}|g_{r}(X_{s})-g_{r}(Y_{s})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}(\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X_{s},Y_{s})|X_{s}-Y_{s}|^{\delta}).

Further, by Hölder’s inequality

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X,Y)|X-Y|^{\delta}\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(X,Y)\|_{L^{pq}_{*{t}}}\||X-Y|^{\delta}\|_{L^{pr}_{*{t}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{L^{pq\lambda}_{*{t}}})+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|Y\|_{L^{pq\lambda}_{*{t}}}))\|X-Y\|_{L^{pr}_{*{t}}}^{\delta},

for all
p\geq 1
and
t\in[0,T]
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
l,\kappa,\lambda\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random field with
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
and
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
jointly measurable.
Then
g(X)\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
with

\|g(X)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}},p}\lesssim\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},2p\lambda})(1\vee\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},2p}^{\gamma}),

uniformly over random fields
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
, and jointly measurable functions
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
.

Proof 4.8
.

By Lemma
12
(b)

\displaystyle\|g(X(x))-g(X(y))\|_{L^{p}_{*}}\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}({\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\|X(x)\|_{L^{2p\lambda}_{*}})+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X(y)\|_{L^{2p\lambda}_{*}}))\|X(x)-X(y)\|_{L^{2p}_{*}}^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}({\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},{2p\lambda}}\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}(x))+\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},{2p\lambda}}\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}(y)))

\displaystyle\cdot\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},{2p\gamma}}^{\gamma}|x-y|^{\delta\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},{2p\lambda}})\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}(x,y)\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},{2p}}^{\gamma}|x-y|^{\delta\gamma},

uniformly over
x,y,X
and
g
.

The following lemma contains a stability property similar to Lemma
9
(e), except for
g
deterministic,
f=X
random, and with index set
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma 14
.

Let
l,\kappa,\lambda\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random field with
X\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
and
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
jointly measurable.
Then
g(X)\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa(\lambda+l)}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
with

\|g(X)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa(\lambda+l)}},p}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},4p\lambda})(1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},4p(1\vee l)}^{\gamma\vee l}),

uniformly over random fields
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}^{d}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}(U,L^{\infty-}_{*})
, and jointly measurable functions
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
.
Further, we have
\mathbb{E}g(X)\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa(\lambda+l)}}(U,L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
with

\|\mathbb{E}g(X)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa(\lambda+l)}}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}},

uniformly over jointly measurable functions
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}\in\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{\Pi e}))
.

Proof 4.9
.

Suppose
l=0
.
By Lemma
12
(a)

\displaystyle\|g(X(x))\|_{L^{p}_{*}}\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X(x)\|_{L^{p\lambda}_{*}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},p\lambda}{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}(x))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},p\lambda})\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}(x),

uniformly over
x,X
and
g
. Together with Lemma
13
, the statement for
l=0
follows.

For
l>0
we proceed similarly to the proof of Lemma
9
(e).
Let
\alpha
be a multi-index with
|\alpha|\leq l
. By Faa di Bruno’s formula (see Proposition
5
(c))

\partial^{\alpha}(g(X))=\sum_{k=1}^{|\alpha|}\sum_{\mathcal{B}}\frac{\alpha!}{\mathcal{B}!}\langle\nabla^{k}g(X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle.

Given a partition
\mathcal{B}
of
\alpha
into
n
multi-indices, we have, using Lemma
9
(a) and (c)

\|\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa l}},p}\lesssim\|\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa n}},p}\lesssim\prod_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\|\partial^{\beta}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},np}\leq\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},np}^{n}\leq 1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},lp}^{l},

uniformly over
X
, for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
. Further, using the previous calculation

\|\nabla^{k}g(X)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}},p}\lesssim\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},2p\lambda})(1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},2p}^{\gamma}),

uniformly over
X
and
g
, for all
p\geq 1
.
Thus, using Lemma
9
(d) with
q=r=2

\displaystyle\|\langle\nabla^{k}g(X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa\lambda}\cdot\mathcal{V}_{\kappa l}},p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla^{k}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}},4p\lambda})(1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},4p}^{\gamma})(1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},2lp}^{l})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\gamma}_{\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}}}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},4p\lambda})(1\vee\|X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l,\delta}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}},4p(1\vee l)}^{\gamma\vee l}),

uniformly over
g
and
X
. By Lemma
8
(b) and Lemma
9
(a), (b), the desired result follows.

Fix a filtration
\mathcal{F}=(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
on
(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{\Omega},\mathbb{P})
satisfying the usual conditions.
We denote by
L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*}
the subset of
L^{\infty-}_{*}
of
\mathcal{F}
-adapted processes, and similarly we define
L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*}
and
L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*{t}}
.

Lemma 15
.

The following estimates hold true.

(a)

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Y_{s}\,ds\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|Y_{s}\|_{p}\,ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}\,ds
, for all
p\geq 1,t\in[0,T]
and
Y\in L^{p}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(b)

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Y_{s}\,ds\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{1/2}
,
uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
and
Y\in L^{p}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for all
p\geq 1
.

(c)

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Z_{s}\,dW_{s}\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|Z_{s}\|_{p}^{2}\,ds\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|Z\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{1/2}
,
uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
and
Z\in L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, for all
p\geq 2
.

(d)

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Y_{s}\,ds+\int_{0}^{\cdot}Z_{s}\,dW_{s}\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}+\|Z\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds
,
uniformly over
t\in[0,T],Y\in L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
Z\in L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*{t}}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, for all
p\geq 2
.

Proof 4.10
.

Let
p\geq 1
and
Y\in L^{p}_{*{t}}
. By Minkowski’s integral inequality we have

\|\|Y\|_{L^{1}([0,t])}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\|\|Y\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|_{L^{1}([0,t])}.

Hence,

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Y_{s}\,ds\|_{L_{p}^{*t}}\leq\|\int_{0}^{t}|Y_{s}|\,ds\|_{p}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|Y_{s}\|_{p}\,ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}\|Y\|_{L_{p}^{*s}}\,ds,

proving (a). Note that

\left(\int_{0}^{t}u_{s}\,ds\right)^{2}\leq t\int_{0}^{t}u_{s}^{2}\,ds\leq T\int_{0}^{t}u_{s}^{2}\,ds,\quad u\in L^{2}([0,t]),t\geq 0,

by Jensen’s inequality.
Thus, (b) follows from (a).

For
p\geq 2
, we have

\|\|Z\|_{L^{2}([0,t])}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\|\|Z\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|_{L^{2}([0,t])},

again by Minkowski’s integral inequality.
Thus, using the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality

\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}Z\,dW\|_{L_{p}^{*t}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}|Z_{s}|^{2}\,ds\right)^{p/2}\right]^{1/p}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}|Z_{s}|^{p}\,ds\right)^{2/p}\right]^{1/2}\leq\left(\int_{0}^{t}\|Z\|_{L_{p}^{*s}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{1/2},

uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
and
Z\in L^{p,\operatorname{ad}}_{*{t}}
. This proves (c).
Using the inequality
(x+y)^{2}\leq 3(x^{2}+y^{2})
, we conclude (d) from (b) and (c).

4.3
Differentiating stochastic differential equations

In the following, we consider family of stochastic differential equations parameterized by a set

\Theta=I_{1}\times\cdots\times I_{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{m-d}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}

for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and bounded intervals
I_{1},\dots,I_{d}
.

Theorem 15
.

Suppose
b\in\operatorname{Lip}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
\sigma\in\operatorname{Lip}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))
, and
\varphi\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*})
. Then, the family of stochastic differential equations

X_{t}=\varphi_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{s}(X_{s})\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}(X_{s})\,dW_{s},\quad t\in[0,T]

admits a unique solution
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
. That is,

X_{t}(x)=\varphi_{t}(x)+\int_{0}^{t}b_{s}(x,X_{s}(x))\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{s}(x,X_{s}(x))\,dW_{s},\quad t\in[0,T]

up to indistinguishability, for all
x\in\Theta
, and given another solution
Y:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

X_{t}(x)=Y_{t}(x),\quad t\in[0,T],

up to indistinguishability, for all
x\in\Theta
.
Further,
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*})
and for all
p\geq 2
there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\|X\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{p}_{*{t}})}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{p}_{*{t}})}e^{c(\|b\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2})},

uniformly over
b\in\operatorname{Lip}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
\sigma\in\operatorname{Lip}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))
,

\varphi\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*})
and
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof 4.11
.

Existence, uniqueness and adaptedness is essentially due to a standard result, cf.
26
for example. The extension from an initial value
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
to a process
\varphi
is discussed in
28
.

We prove
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-}_{*})
and the inequality.
Let
p\geq 2
. By Lemma
15
(d) and Lemma
12
(b) with
q=\infty
and
r=1

\displaystyle\|X(x)-X(y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|b(X(x))-b(X(y))\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|\sigma(X(x))-\sigma(X(y))\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{p}_{*{t}})}^{2}|x-y|^{2\delta}+(\|b\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2})\int_{0}^{t}\|X(x)-X(y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds,

(17)

uniformly over
x,y,t,b,\sigma
and
\varphi
. Grönwall’s inequality implies

\|X(x)-X(y)\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}\lesssim\|\varphi\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{p}_{*{t}})}^{2}e^{c(\|b\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}+\|\sigma\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2})}|x-y|^{2\gamma},

uniformly over
x,y,t,b,\sigma
and
\varphi
, for some
c>0
.
Taking the square root gives the desired estimate. Note that the estimate for large
p
(here
p\geq 2
) suffices to show
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-}_{*})
.

Theorem 15
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\delta\in(0,1]
with
l+\delta\geq 1
,
b\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}))
,
\sigma\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))
, and
\varphi\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*})
. Let
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the unique solution to the family of stochastic differential equations

X_{t}=\varphi_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}b_{u}(\cdot,X_{u})\,du+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{u}(\cdot,X_{u})\,dW_{u},\quad t\in[0,T].

Then,

X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d},(\omega,t,x)\mapsto X_{t}(x)\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*}).

Further, for every multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|\leq l
,
\partial^{\alpha}X
satisfies the stochastic differential equation

\partial^{\alpha}X_{t}=\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\partial^{\alpha}(b_{u}(\cdot,X_{u}))\,du+\int_{0}^{t}\partial^{\alpha}(\sigma_{u}(\cdot,X_{u}))\,dW_{u},\quad t\geq 0.

Proof 4.12
.

By extending
b,\sigma
and
\varphi
to an open neighborhood of
\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we can assume wlog that
\Theta
is open.
To show
X\in\mathcal{C}^{l}
and SDE representation of
\partial^{\alpha}X
cf.
26
Theorem 3.4. They cover the case
l=1
and, where the dependence is only on the initial condition. The generalization to our case is straightforward (although cumbersome).

We show
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}
by proving inductively: For all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
k\leq l
we have
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}
, where

\gamma=\begin{cases}1,&k<l,\\
\delta,&k=l.\end{cases}

By Theorem
15
,
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}=\operatorname{Lip}^{\gamma}
and so the statement is true for
k=0
. Now, assume
k\in\mathbb{N},k\leq l
and that
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}
.
Recall that

\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}=\{f\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{k,\gamma}:\nabla^{m}f\in\operatorname{Lip},m=0,\dots,k-1\}=\{f\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{k,\gamma}:f\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}\}.

Thus, it suffices to prove
X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{k,\gamma}
or equivalently
\partial^{\alpha}X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}
for all multi-indices
|\alpha|
of size
k
.

Let
\alpha
be a multi-index of size
k
.
We write
f^{1}=b,f^{2}=\sigma,W^{1}_{t}=t
and
W^{2}=W
in the following.
Firstly, note that Faa di Bruno’s formula implies

\partial^{\alpha}f^{i}(\cdot,X)=\sum_{\beta\leq\alpha}\sum_{j=0}^{|\alpha-\beta|}\sum_{\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\alpha-\beta}}\frac{(\alpha-\beta)!}{\mathcal{B}!}\langle\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}(\cdot,X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle.

Here, we denote derivatives with respect to the two coordinates of
f^{i}
with indices
x
and
y
respectively.
For most of the terms of the form
\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X
, we can use the inductive assumption to estimate the corresponding summands above. The only exception is for
|\beta|=0
and
j=1
. In this case
|\mathcal{S}^{\alpha-\beta}_{j}|=1
and the inner sum is given by the sole summand

\langle\nabla_{y}f^{i}(\cdot,X),\partial^{\alpha}X\rangle.

That is, only in this case does the inner sum depend on the left-hand side of the differential equation.
Define

\psi_{t}^{i}=\int_{0}^{t}(\partial^{\alpha}(f^{i}_{u}(\cdot,X_{u}))-\langle\nabla_{y}f^{i}_{u}(\cdot,X_{u}),\partial^{\alpha}X_{u}\rangle)\,dW^{i}_{u},\quad i=1,2,

Then

\partial^{\alpha}X_{t}=\partial^{\alpha}\varphi_{t}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\psi_{t}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\nabla_{y}f_{u}^{i}(X_{u}),\partial^{\alpha}X_{u}\rangle\,dW^{i}_{u},\quad t\in[0,T],

(18)

and only the last summand depends on
\partial^{\alpha}X
.
Now, let
p\geq 1
.

Estimating
\psi
:

By defining

A(\alpha)=\{(\beta,j,\mathcal{B}):\beta\leq\alpha,j\in\{0,\dots,|\alpha-\beta|\},\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{\alpha-\beta},|\beta|\geq 1\text{ or }j\geq 2\}

we can write

\psi^{i}_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{(\beta,j,\mathcal{B})\in A(\alpha)}\frac{(\alpha-\beta)!}{\mathcal{B}!}\langle\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}(\cdot,X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle\,dW^{i}_{u},\quad t\geq 0.

Let
(\beta,j,\mathcal{B})\in A(\alpha)
. Note that
\alpha\notin\mathcal{B}
and so
|\tilde{\beta}|<k
for all
\tilde{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}
.
Note that by Lemma
13

\displaystyle\|g(X)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},p}\leq

\displaystyle\|g(X)\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma},p}\vee\|g(X)\|_{\mathcal{C}_{b},p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},4p})\vee\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},4p}),

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],E))
with
E\in\{\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\}
. Further, by Lemma
7

\max_{1\leq m\leq k}\|\nabla^{m}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\lesssim\max_{1\leq m<k}\|\nabla^{m}g\|_{\operatorname{Lip}_{b}}\vee\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k,\gamma}_{b}}\asymp\max_{m<k}\|\nabla^{m}g\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\vee\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k,\gamma}_{b}}\asymp\|g\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],E)),E\in\{\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\}
.
Similarly
\max_{1\leq m\leq k-1}\|\nabla^{m}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\lesssim\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k}}
, uniformly over
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}
.
Thus, by Lemma
9
(c) and (d)

\displaystyle\|\langle\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}(\cdot,X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},p}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}(\cdot,X)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},2p}\|\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},2p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\|\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},8p})\prod_{\tilde{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}}\|\partial^{\tilde{\beta}}X\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},2pj}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f^{i}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},8p})\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},2pj}^{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f^{i}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},8pk}^{k}),

uniformly over
f^{i}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d^{\times i}}))
and
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}
, for
i=1,2
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\|\psi^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},p}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{(\beta,j,\mathcal{B})\in A(\alpha)}\|\langle\partial^{\beta}_{x}\nabla^{j}_{y}f^{i}(X),\partial^{\mathcal{B}}X\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b},p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f^{i}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},8pk}^{k}),

uniformly over
f^{i}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k+\gamma}
and
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}
, for
i=1,2
.

Estimating
\int\langle\nabla_{y}f(X),\partial^{\alpha}X\rangle
:

For
g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],E)),E\in\{\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\}
, we have

\displaystyle|\langle g_{t}(x,y),z\rangle-\langle g_{t}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),z^{\prime}\rangle|\leq

\displaystyle|\langle g_{t}(x,y)-g_{t}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),z\rangle-\langle g_{t}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime}),z-z^{\prime}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}|z|\left|\begin{pmatrix}x-x^{\prime}\\
y-y^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right|^{\gamma}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}|z-z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}(|z||x-x^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+|z||y-y^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+|z-z^{\prime}|),

for
x,x^{\prime}\in\Theta,y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
z,z^{\prime}\in E
.
Hence, using the Hölder inequality

\displaystyle\|\langle g(x,X(x)),\partial^{\alpha}X(x)\rangle-\langle g(x^{\prime},X(x^{\prime})),\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\rangle\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)\|_{L^{2p}_{*{t}}}(|x-x^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+\|X(x)-X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{2p\gamma}_{*{t}}}^{\gamma})+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\|\partial^{\alpha}X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{b},2p}(1+\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},2p}^{\gamma})|x-x^{\prime}|^{\gamma}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}},

uniformly over
t\in[0,T],x,x^{\prime}\in\Theta,g\in\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}
and
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}\cap\mathcal{C}^{k}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
7

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}X\|_{\mathcal{C}_{b},2p}(1+\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},2p}^{\gamma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k-1}X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},2p}(1+\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip},2p}^{\gamma})\lesssim 1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},2p}^{1+\gamma},

uniformly over
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
15
(c)

\displaystyle\|\int_{0}^{\cdot}\langle\nabla_{y}f_{u}^{i}(x,X_{u}(x)),\partial^{\alpha}X_{u}(x)\rangle-\langle\nabla_{y}f_{u}^{i}(x^{\prime},X_{u}(x^{\prime})),\partial^{\alpha}X_{u}(x^{\prime})\rangle\,dW^{i}_{u}\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle\nabla_{y}f^{i}(x,X(x)),\partial^{\alpha}X(x)\rangle-\langle\nabla_{y}f^{i}(x^{\prime},X(x^{\prime})),\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\rangle\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{y}f^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},2p}^{1+\gamma})^{2}|x-x^{\prime}|^{2\gamma}+\|\nabla_{y}f^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds,

uniformly over
t\in[0,T],x,x^{\prime}\in\Theta,f^{i}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{1+\delta}_{b}
and
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}\cap\mathcal{C}^{k}
, for
i=1,2
.

Finishing the inductive step

By Equation (
18
) and Lemma
15

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}(\|\psi^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{y}f^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}(1\vee\|X\|_{\operatorname{Lip}^{k},2p}^{1+\gamma})^{2})|x-x^{\prime}|^{2\gamma}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|\nabla_{y}f^{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0,\gamma}_{b}}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|x-x^{\prime}|^{2\gamma}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{s}}}^{2}\,ds,

uniformly over
t\in[0,T],x,x^{\prime}\in\Theta
and
X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}\cap\mathcal{C}^{k}
.
Thus, by Grönwall’s inequality

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}X(x)-\partial^{\alpha}X(x^{\prime})\|_{L^{p}_{*{t}}}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle|x-x|^{2\gamma}\text{ uniformly over }x,x^{\prime}\in\Theta,X\in\operatorname{Lip}^{k}\cap\mathcal{C}^{k}.

Hence,
\partial^{\alpha}X\in\dot{\mathcal{C}}^{\gamma}
for all multi-indices
\alpha
of size
k
, as desired.

Using Theorem
15
we can deduce the smoothness of solutions of SDEs in the initial condition
x
, the initial time point
s
and a small parameter
\varepsilon
.

Corollary 16
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\delta\in(0,1],\Theta:=[0,1]\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
and suppose we are given functions

\displaystyle b:

\displaystyle\,[0,T]\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d},(t,\varepsilon,x)\mapsto b^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x)\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d})),

\displaystyle\sigma:

\displaystyle\,[0,T]\times[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d},(t,\varepsilon,x)\mapsto\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{t}(x)\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})).

Let
X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the unique solution to the family of stochastic differential equations

X_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x)=x+\int_{s}^{t}b_{u}^{\varepsilon}(X_{u}^{\varepsilon,s}(x))\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{u}^{\varepsilon}(X_{u}^{\varepsilon,s}(x))\,dW_{u},\quad t\in[0,T],

with the convention that
\int_{s}^{t}\,du=\int_{s}^{t}\,dW_{u}=0
for
s>t
.
Then,

X:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to\mathbb{R}^{d},(\omega,t,(\varepsilon,s,x))\mapsto X_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x)\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l+\delta}(\Theta,L^{\infty-,\operatorname{ad}}_{*}).

Further, for all
k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and every multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|\leq l-k-m
,
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{k}\partial_{s}^{m}X
satisfies the stochastic differential equation

\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{k}\partial_{s}^{m}X_{t}=\varphi^{\alpha,k,m}+\int_{s}^{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{k}\partial_{s}^{m}(b_{u}^{\varepsilon}(X_{u}^{\varepsilon,s}))\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{k}\partial_{s}^{m}(\sigma_{u}^{\varepsilon}(X_{u}^{\varepsilon,s}))\,dW_{u},\quad t\in[0,T].

Here,

\varphi^{\alpha,k,m}(x)=\begin{cases}x,&|\alpha|=k=m=0,\\
e_{j}&\alpha=\{j\},k=m=0,\\
0,&|\alpha|>1\text{ or }k+m>0,\end{cases}\quad x\in\Theta.

Proof 4.13
.

We augment the state space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by time.
Define

\tilde{b}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
b_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)\end{pmatrix},\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}(t,x)=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
\sigma_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)\end{pmatrix},\quad t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Consider the family of stochastic differential equations

\tilde{X}_{t}(\varepsilon,s,x)=\begin{pmatrix}s\\
x\end{pmatrix}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{b}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{X}_{u}(\varepsilon,s,x))\,du+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\sigma}^{\varepsilon}(\tilde{X}_{u}(\varepsilon,s,x))\,dW_{u}.

The assumptions of Theorem
15
are satisfied for this system.
Write
\tilde{X}=(Y,Z)
with
Y
one-dimensional.
By construction,
Y_{t}(\varepsilon,s,x)=s+t
. Thus,

\displaystyle\tilde{X}_{t}(\varepsilon,s,x)=\begin{pmatrix}s+t\\
Z_{t}(\varepsilon,s,x)\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}s+t\\
x+\int_{0}^{t}b_{u+s}^{\varepsilon}(Z_{u}(\varepsilon,s,x))\,du+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{u+s}^{\varepsilon}(Z_{u}(\varepsilon,s,x))\,dW_{u}\end{pmatrix},

and so
Z_{t-s}(\varepsilon,s,x)=X_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x)
, up to indistinguishability in
t\in[0,T]
, for all
(\varepsilon,s,x)\in\Theta
. Thus, the desired properties for
X
follow from the ones for
\tilde{X}
.

5
Stochastic modified equations

5.1
Introduction and main results

For the remainder of this chapter, we write
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}:=\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}}
, where
\mathcal{V}_{\kappa}(x)=1+x^{\kappa}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}:=\mathcal{C}^{0}_{\kappa}
.
We work again in the setting of Subsection
2.2
. In addition to
f,\bar{f}
and
\Sigma
, we consider functions
b:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
D:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
.

Assumption 17
.

D_{t}(x)
is a symmetric positive semi-definite matrix for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
We have
b\in\operatorname{Lip}^{5+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sqrt{D}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{5+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
.

By Assumption (A
17
), we have
\sqrt{D}\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{1}
. Hence,
D\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{2}
by Lemma
2.1
(iii).
For all
h\in[0,1]
we consider the following family of stochastic differential equations

\displaystyle dX_{t}^{h}=\bar{f}_{t}(X^{h}_{t})+hb_{t}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt+\sqrt{hD_{t}(X^{h}_{t})}\,dW_{t},\quad t\in[0,T].

(19)

Notice that, as
h\downarrow 0
, Equation (
19
) becomes the ODE

\partial_{t}X_{t}^{0}=\bar{f}_{t}(X^{0}_{t}),\quad t\in[0,T],

which we investigated in Section
3
.
Denote by
X^{h,t}(x)
the (essentially) unique solution of (
19
) with
X_{t}^{h,t}(x)=x
and set
X_{r}^{h,t}(x)=x
for
r<t
.
Given
g\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we define

v^{g}:[0,1]\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R},(h,r,t,x)\mapsto v^{g,h,r}_{t}(x):=\mathbb{E}g(X_{r}^{h,t}(x)).

We also write
v^{g,h}=v^{g,h,T}
and
v^{h}=v^{g,h}
if the choice of
g
is clear from the context.
Notice that
v^{g,0}_{t}(x)=g(X^{0,t}_{T}(x))
.

Theorem 5.0
.

Assume (A
1
) and (A
17
).
Then for all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists a function
\rho^{g}:T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)\to\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),h\mapsto\rho^{g,h}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{T/h}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{T}^{h})=\frac{1}{2}h\int_{0}^{T}(\langle\nabla^{2}v^{g,0},\Sigma-D\rangle-\langle\nabla v^{g,0},2b+\nabla^{\intercal}\bar{f}\bar{f}+\partial_{t}\bar{f}\rangle)_{t}(X_{t}^{0})\,dt+h^{2}\rho^{g,h},

(20)

and

\|\rho^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}}

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.

By choosing
b=0
and
D=\Sigma
we recover the first-order stochastic modified equation introduced by
27
, with the difference that our
f
can be time-dependent and does not need to be a gradient field.
Alternatively, we may choose a state-independent diffusion coefficient such as
\Sigma(x^{*})
for some
x^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The resulting equation is easier to work with, but may still provide important insight. Moreover, in some cases setting
(b,D)=(0,\Sigma(x^{*}))
gives us a smaller absolute linear error term than setting
(b,D)=(0,\Sigma)
(see Chapter
5
).

By choosing
b=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{\intercal}\bar{f}\bar{f}+\partial_{t}\bar{f}
and
D=\Sigma
we recover the second-order stochastic modified equation introduced by
27
.

Corollary 18
.

Assume (A
1
),
\bar{f}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{6+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\sqrt{\Sigma}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{6+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
. Suppose
X
is the solution to the family of stochastic differential equation

dX_{t}^{h}=\left(\bar{f}_{t}-\frac{1}{2}h(\nabla^{\intercal}\bar{f}_{t}\bar{f}_{t}+\partial_{t}\bar{f}_{t})\right)(X_{t}^{h})\,dt+\sqrt{h\Sigma(X_{t}^{h})}\,dW_{t},\quad t\in[0,T].

(21)

Then for all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have

\|\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{T/h}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{T}^{h})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim h^{2}\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.

Proof 5.1
.

Apply Theorem
17
in the case
b=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{\intercal}\bar{f}\bar{f}+\partial_{t}\bar{f}
and
D=\Sigma
.

Corollary
18
gives a nice interpretation of the linear error term in Equation (
19
). For general (stochastic) modified equations of the form (
19
) it measures how much our approximation differs from the second-order SME (
21
).

5.2
Proof of Theorem
17

As in Subsection
3
, the linear error term in Equation (
20
) is determined by a linear operator

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathcal{C}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})

which is now given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}w:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\langle\nabla^{2}w,\bar{f}^{\otimes 2}+\Sigma-D\rangle-\langle\nabla w,b\rangle+\langle\partial_{t}\nabla w,\bar{f}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}^{2}w,\quad w\in\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}).

(22)

We write
\mathcal{F}_{t}w(x):=(\mathcal{F}w)(t,x)
for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. By Lemma
2
, we have

\mathcal{F}{v^{g,0}}=\frac{1}{2}\langle\nabla^{2}v^{g,0},\Sigma-D\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle\nabla v^{g,0},2b+\nabla^{\intercal}\bar{f}\bar{f}+\partial_{t}\bar{f}\rangle,

for all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma
3.2
, we can show

\|\mathcal{F}w\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+2}}\lesssim\|w\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}},\text{ uniformly over }w\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}),

for all
\kappa,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
with
l\leq 5
.

Note that given
g\in\mathcal{C}^{2}
and
r\in[0,T]
, the function
v=v^{g,h,r}
satisfies the Kolmogorov backward equation

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v+\langle\nabla v,\bar{f}\rangle+h\langle\nabla v,b\rangle+\frac{1}{2}h\langle\nabla^{2}v,D\rangle=0,&t\in[0,r],\\
v_{r}=g,\end{cases}

(23)

for all
h\in[0,1]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For all
l\leq 5
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have
v^{g,h}\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}))
, uniformly in
h\in[0,1]
, with

\|v^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}))}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
h\in[0,1]
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}
.
Moreover,

\|v^{g,0}-v^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}))}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}
and
h\in[0,1]
, for
l=0,1,2
.

Proof 5.3
.

We apply Corollary
16
to the family of stochastic differential equations (
19
) with
\varepsilon=\sqrt{h}
.
More precisely, let

\Theta=\{(\varepsilon,s,x):\varepsilon\in[0,1],s\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}.

We consider the
\Theta\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
-indexed family of stochastic differential equations

dY_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x)=\bar{f}_{t}(Y_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x))+\varepsilon^{2}b_{t}(Y_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x))\,dt+\varepsilon\sigma_{t}(Y_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x))\,dW_{t},\quad t\in[s,T],

with
Y_{t}^{\varepsilon,s}(x)=x
for
t\in[0,s]
. Here,
\sigma=\sqrt{D}
.
By Corollary
16
, we have
Y\in\operatorname{Lip}^{5+1}([0,1]\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty-}_{*})
.
In particular,
Y\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{1}([0,1]\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty-}_{*})
. Thus, by Lemma
14
, we have
\mathbb{E}g(Y)\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}([0,1]\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{d}))
for all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}
, with

\|\mathbb{E}g(Y)\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}},\text{ uniformly over }g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),

for every
l\leq 5
.
In particular,

v^{g,h}=\mathbb{E}g(X^{h,\cdot})=\mathbb{E}g(Y^{\sqrt{h},\cdot})\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}),

uniformly in
h\in[0,1]
, with

\|v^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
h\in[0,1]
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}
, for every
l\leq 5
.

Next, we consider derivatives of
Y^{\varepsilon}
in
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. By Corollary
16
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon,s}_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon^{2}b_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}))\,du+\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}))\,dW_{u}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}2\varepsilon b_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon^{2}\langle\nabla b(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}\rangle\,dt+\int_{s}^{t}\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon})+\varepsilon\langle\nabla\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}\rangle\,dW_{u}

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\varepsilon,s}_{t}=

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}2b_{u}(Y^{\varepsilon}_{u})+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)\,du+\int_{s}^{t}2\langle\nabla\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}Y_{u}^{\varepsilon}\rangle+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon)\,dW_{u}.

Note that

(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon})|_{\varepsilon=0}=\int_{s}^{\cdot}\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{0})\,dW_{u}

is a martingale, as
\sigma(Y^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}_{*}
and so
\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}|\sigma_{u}(Y_{u}^{\varepsilon})|^{2}\,du<\infty
. In particular,
\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon})]|_{\varepsilon=0}=0
and further

\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla g(Y^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}\rangle]|_{\varepsilon=0}=\langle\nabla g(Y^{0}),\mathbb{E}[\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}]|_{\varepsilon=0}\rangle=0,

for all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
.
Let
l\in\{0,1,2\}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}
. By Taylor’s theorem and Lemma
10

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}g(Y^{\varepsilon})=

\displaystyle g(Y^{0})+\varepsilon\partial_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{E}g(Y^{\varepsilon}))|_{\varepsilon=0}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}(\mathbb{E}g(Y^{\varepsilon}))|_{\varepsilon=\xi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(Y^{0})+\varepsilon\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla g(Y^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon}\rangle]|_{\varepsilon=0}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\varepsilon}),(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\varepsilon})^{\otimes 2}\rangle]|_{\varepsilon=\xi}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla g(Y^{\varepsilon}),\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\varepsilon}\rangle]|_{\varepsilon=\xi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(Y^{0})+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\mathbb{E}[\langle\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\xi}),(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\xi})^{\otimes 2}\rangle+\langle\nabla g(Y^{\xi}),\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\xi}\rangle],

(24)

for some
\xi\in(0,1)
.
Since
Y\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{1}
, we have
\langle\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\xi}),(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\xi})^{\otimes 2}\rangle,\langle\nabla g(Y^{\xi}),\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\xi}\rangle\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+l+2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty-}_{*})
, with

\displaystyle\|\langle\nabla g(Y^{\xi}),\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\xi}\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+2},1}\lesssim\|\langle\nabla g(Y^{\xi}),\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\xi}\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+1},1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla g(Y^{\xi})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l},2}\|\partial_{\varepsilon}^{2}Y^{\xi}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{1},2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}},

\displaystyle\|\langle\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\xi}),(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\xi})^{\otimes 2}\rangle\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+2},1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\xi})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l},2}\|(\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\xi})^{\otimes 2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{2},2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}g(Y^{\xi})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l},2}\|\partial_{\varepsilon}Y^{\xi}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{1},2}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa}},\text{ uniformly over }g\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa},

by Lemma
9
and
14
. Hence, by Lemma
11
and Equation (
5.3
)

\|v^{g,0}-v^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+2}}=\|g(Y^{0})-\mathbb{E}g(Y^{\sqrt{h}})\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+2}}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}
and
h\in[0,1]

Lemma 19
.

Given
g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}
there exists a function
\xi^{g}:(T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1))\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{n}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{nh}^{h})=h^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{kh}[v^{g,h}](\chi_{k}^{h})]+h^{2}\xi^{g,h}_{n},\quad h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1),

and

\|\xi^{g,h}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa},h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
and
n\in\{0,\dots,T/h\}
.

Proof 5.4
.

We follow the proof of Lemma
3
. Note that for
g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we have
v^{g,h,s}\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, uniformly in
h\in[0,1]
and
s\in[0,T]
, with

\sup_{h\in[0,1]}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\|v^{g,h,s}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa+3}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\text{ uniformly over }g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa},

(25)

by Lemma
5.2
.
A Taylor expansion of
v^{h}:=v^{g,h,T}
yields

\mathbb{E}v_{(k+1)h}^{h}(\chi_{k+1}^{h})-\mathbb{E}v_{kh}^{h}(\chi_{k}^{h})=hA_{1}^{h}+h^{2}A_{2}^{h}+\mathbb{E}\zeta^{h}_{k,n},

as before, except with

\displaystyle A_{1}^{h}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{t}v_{kh}^{h}+\langle\bar{f}_{kh},\nabla v_{kh}^{h}\rangle+h\langle b_{kh},\nabla v_{kh}^{h}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}h\langle D_{kh},\nabla^{2}v_{kh}^{h}\rangle)(\chi_{k}^{h})]=0,

by Equation (
23
).
To compensate for the
h\langle b_{kh},\nabla v_{kh}^{h}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}h\langle D_{t},\nabla^{2}v_{kh}^{h}\rangle
term we have

\displaystyle A_{2}^{h}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{t}^{2}v_{kh}^{h}+2\langle\bar{f}_{kh},\partial_{t}\nabla v_{kh}^{h}\rangle-2\langle b_{kh},\nabla v_{kh}^{h}\rangle+\langle\Sigma_{kh}-D_{kh}+\bar{f}_{kh}^{\otimes 2},\nabla^{2}v_{kh}^{h}\rangle)(\chi_{k}^{h})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{kh}[v^{h}](\chi_{k}^{h})].

Using Inequality (
25
) we deduce that
\xi^{g,h}_{n}:=h^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}\zeta_{k,n}^{h}
satisfies

\|\xi_{n}^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa},h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
and
n\in\{0,\dots,T/h\}
.

We do an initial approximation just as in the ODE case.

Lemma 5.5
.

For all
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists a function
\rho^{g}:T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)\to\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),h\mapsto\rho^{g,h}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{T/h}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{T}^{h})=h\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{g,h}](X_{t}^{h})]\,dt+h^{2}\rho^{g,h},

(26)

and

\|\rho^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}}

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.

Proof 5.6
.

The proof is analogous to the proof of Theorem
1
. Let
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}
and define
\varphi^{h}=\mathcal{F}v^{h},h\in[0,1]
. Lemma
19
implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}(\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{T/h}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{T}^{h}))=

\displaystyle h\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(\chi_{n}^{h})+h\xi_{T/h}^{g,h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt+h\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(\chi_{n}^{h})-\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}h\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt+h\xi_{T/h}^{g,h},

with

\|\xi_{T/h}^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}},\text{ uniformly over }g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa},h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1).

Note that
\varphi^{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, uniformly in
h\in[0,1]
, with

\|\varphi^{h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l}_{\kappa+l+4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|v^{h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa+l+2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(27)

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l+2}_{\kappa}
and
h\in[0,1]
, for all
l\leq 3
.
Thus, we may use Lemma
19
again to estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}|\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(\chi_{n}^{h})-\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})|\leq

\displaystyle h^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{kh}[v^{\varphi_{nh}^{h},h}](\chi_{k}^{h})]+\hat{\xi}^{g,h}_{n}|,

where by Inequality (
27
)

\|\hat{\xi}^{g,h}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim\|\varphi^{h}_{nh}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa+7}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa},h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
and
n\in\{0,\dots,T/h\}
.
Since by Lemma
2.3
, Lemma
5.2
and Inequality (
27
),

\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{kh}[v^{\varphi_{nh}^{h},h}](\chi_{k}^{h})]\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+10}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{F}_{kh}[v^{\varphi_{nh}^{h},h}]\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+10}}\lesssim\|v^{\varphi_{nh}^{h},h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+8}}\lesssim\|\varphi_{nh}^{h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+6}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1),k\leq n\in\{0,\dots,T/h\},g\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}
, we conclude

h\|\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(\chi_{n}^{h})-\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.
Further, approximating the integral
\int\varphi\,dt
by a left Riemann sum yields

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}h\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}hT\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\partial_{t}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})|,

Thus, by Lemma
14
and Inequality (
27
)

\|\sum_{n=0}^{\frac{T}{h}-1}h\mathbb{E}\varphi_{nh}^{h}(X_{nh}^{h})-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}}\lesssim h\|\mathbb{E}\varphi^{h}(X^{h})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+6}}\lesssim\|\varphi^{h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{\kappa+5}}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.
Putting all estimates together yields

\mathbb{E}g(\chi_{T/h}^{h})-\mathbb{E}g(X_{T}^{h})=h\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\varphi_{t}^{h}(X_{t}^{h})\,dt+h^{2}\rho^{g,h},

with

\|\rho^{g,h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+13}}\lesssim\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{5}_{\kappa}
and
h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
.

Proof 5.7

(Proof of Theorem
17
)
.

Firstly, Lemma
5.2
implies

\|v_{t}^{h}-v_{t}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

and also

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{0})-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{h})]\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=

\displaystyle\|v_{t}^{\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}],0}-v_{t}^{\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}],h}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h\|\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}]\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+6}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h\|v^{h}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa+4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa},h\in T/\mathbb{N}\cap(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
.
Thus, by Lemma
2.1
(v)

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{h})]-\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{0}](X_{t}^{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{h})]-\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}}

\displaystyle+\|\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}-v^{0}](X_{t}^{0})\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h(\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}}+\|\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}-v^{0}]\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h(\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}}+\|v_{t}^{h}-v_{t}^{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\kappa+6}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa},h\in[0,1]
and
t\in[0,T]
.
Hence,

\|\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{0}](X_{t}^{0})\,dt-\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{t}[v^{h}](X_{t}^{h})]\,dt\|_{\mathcal{C}_{\kappa+8}}\lesssim h\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}},

uniformly over
g\in\mathcal{C}^{4}_{\kappa}
and
h\in[0,1]
.
Consequently, Theorem
17
follows from Lemma
5.5
.

Chapter 4
SMEs for optimization and learning

So far we have discussed (stochastic) modified equations as continuous-time models for numerical methods of differential equations. In this brief chapter, we shift our focus towards stochastic gradient optimization, statistical learning, and their relation to stochastic modified equations.
This chapter is adapted from and expands on
6
.

6
Stochastic optimization and SGD

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Given a function
\mathcal{R}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)\in\mathcal{C}^{1}
, which we call the
objective
or
risk
, we consider the following optimization problem

\min_{\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{R}(\theta).

To solve this problem, consider the following ODE

\dot{X}_{t}^{0}=-\nabla\mathcal{R}(X_{t}^{0}).

(28)

Under certain assumptions
X^{0}
exists and it converges to a critical point
\theta^{*}
of
\mathcal{R}
, that is
\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta^{*})=0
. We also call
X^{0}

gradient flow
.
In some situations
\theta^{*}
is indeed the global minimum of
\mathcal{R}
, for example if
\mathcal{R}
is strongly convex with Lipschitz gradient.
In any case, if we wanted to find
\theta^{*}
, one possible avenue would be applying the Euler method with step size
h\in(0,1)
to gradient flow:

\chi_{n+1}=\chi_{n}-h\nabla\mathcal{R}(\chi_{n}).

This resulting algorithm is called
gradient descent
. Note that our goal here has shifted compared to the numerics of differential equations.
It is not necessary for gradient descent to be very close to gradient flow. The only important thing for us is that it (approximately) minimizes
\mathcal{R}
. Our main interest is now in gradient descent and we view gradient flow as continuous-time approximation to it (instead of thinking of approximating the other way around).

Consider now a family of functions

R:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty),(\theta,z)\mapsto R_{z}(\theta)

and a probability measure
\nu
on a measurable space
\mathcal{Z}
. We may call
R_{z}
the risk at the point
z\in\mathcal{Z}
. Suppose
\mathcal{R}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{z\sim\nu}[R_{z}(\theta)]
. Under mild assumptions we have

\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}_{z\sim\nu}[\nabla R_{z}(\theta)],\quad\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

In practice, it may be very costly or even impossible to compute the full gradient
\nabla\mathcal{R}
.
In this case, we can instead apply a noisy Euler method:

\chi_{n+1}=\chi_{n}-h\nabla R_{\bm{z}(n)}(\chi_{n})

(29)

where
\bm{z}(n)\in\mathcal{Z}
with
\bm{z}(n)\sim\nu
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
This resulting algorithm is called
stochastic gradient descent
.

We think of
(\bm{z}(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
as a sequence of data points.
We highlight two conceptually different scenarios.

(a)

Suppose
\nu
is an empirical measure, that is there exist
z_{1}^{\prime},\dots,z_{N}^{\prime}
such that
\nu
is given by the average of the Dirac measures

\nu=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathds{1}_{\cdot}(z_{n}^{\prime}).

Then we can think of
\nu
as a finite data set, or
sample
of size
N
, and the SGD method uses this sample to minimize the
empirical risk
or
training error

\mathcal{R}(\theta)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}R_{z_{n}^{\prime}}(\theta),\quad\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

If
(\bm{z}(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is i.i.d. with
\bm{z}(0)\sim\nu
, then we call (
29
) SGD
with replacement
. That is because we can think of
(\bm{z}(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
as sampling uniformly from an urn containing
z_{1}^{\prime},\dots,z_{N}^{\prime}
and after sampling the urn is refilled.

(b)

Suppose
\nu
is a non-atomic measure. Then we can think of
\nu
as a
population
, that is the true real-world process by which data
z\sim\nu
is generated. In this case the SGD method is used to minimize the
population risk

\mathcal{R}
(which is usually measured by the
test error
).
If
(\bm{z}(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is i.i.d.  then we call (
29
)
one-pass
SGD. We sample from
\nu
and every sampled data point is only passed over once. In this setting we essentially assume that we are given an infinite sequence of distinct data points (which is true almost surely since
\nu
is non-atomic).

The goal of learning is ultimately to (approximately) minimize the population risk. Empirical risk minimization (ERM) is a proxy task that we can perform when we only have a finite amount of data. However, the result can in some cases deviate greatly from population risk minimization (for example via
“overfitting”
in neural networks).
Instead of focusing abstractly on ERM, we focus our analysis on the actual optimization algorithm SGD.

7
Running examples

7.1
Mini-batch SGD with learning rate schedule

Let
T>0
.
Consider a risk minimization problem
(R:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathcal{Z}\to[0,\infty),\nu)
on a measurable space
\mathcal{Z}
. Fix a batch size
B\in\mathbb{N}
and an i.i.d. sequence
(\bm{z}(n))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathcal{Z}
with
\bm{z}(n)\sim\nu
. We consider
mini-batch SGD
with
batch size

B
and
learning rate schedule

u:[0,T]\to[0,1]
, given by

\chi_{n+1}^{h}=\chi_{n}^{h}-u_{nh}\frac{h}{B}\sum_{k=0}^{B-1}\nabla R_{\bm{z}({k+Bn})}(\chi_{n}^{h}),\quad h\in(0,1),n\in\{0,\dots,\left\lfloor T/h\right\rfloor\}

(30)

The learning rate in the
n
-th step is
hu_{nh}
, and
h
is interpreted as the
maximal learning rate
.
We want to prove that under certain assumptions Equation (
30
) is a one-step method in the sense of Chapter
3
satisfying Assumption (A
1
).

Let
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{m}
be bounded by a constant
C
and
\bm{z}
be a
\mathcal{Z}
-valued random variable.

Lemma 20
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
and
g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{k}))
with
\nabla g\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}))
. Then
g_{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{k}))
with
\nabla g_{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}))
. In particular,
\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
\nabla\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times k})
and

\partial^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}=\mathbb{E}\partial^{\alpha}g_{\bm{z}},\quad|\alpha|\leq l.

Proof 7.1
.

Consider the linear operator

A:L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{k})\to L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{k})

given by

(Af)(\omega)=f(\bm{z}(\omega)),\quad f\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{k}),\omega\in\Omega.

Indeed,
A
is well-defined, since
f=\tilde{f}
, a.e. on
\mathcal{Z}
implies
f(\bm{z})=\tilde{f}(\bm{z})
almost surely.
Linearity is obvious. Moreover,

\|Af\|_{\infty}\leq\|f\|_{\infty},\quad f\in L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{k}),

so
A
is bounded. In particular,
A\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{k}),L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{k}))
in the sense of Fréchet differentiability. Hence,
g_{\bm{z}}=A\circ g\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{k}))\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty-}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{k}))
with
\partial^{\alpha}g_{\bm{z}}=(\partial^{\alpha}g)_{\bm{z}},|\alpha|\leq l
. Therefore,

\|\partial^{\alpha}g_{\bm{z}}(x)-\partial^{\alpha}g_{\bm{z}}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\|\partial^{\alpha}g_{\cdot}(x)-\partial^{\alpha}g_{\cdot}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})}\lesssim|x-y|,

uniformly over
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for all
|\alpha|\leq l
, i.e.
\nabla g_{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}))
.
By Lemma
10
and Lemma
11
we conclude
\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{k})
with
\nabla\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{l-1,1}_{b}=\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times k})
, and

\partial^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}g_{\bm{z}}=\mathbb{E}\partial^{\alpha}g_{\bm{z}},\quad|\alpha|\leq l.

Lemma 21
.

Let
T>0
and
B,l\in\mathbb{N}
.
Suppose
u:[0,T]\to[0,1]\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}
and
R\in\mathcal{C}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}))
with
\nabla R\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{R}^{d}))
. Define

\displaystyle F(h,t,\theta,z):=

\displaystyle-\frac{u_{t}}{B}\sum_{k=0}^{B-1}\nabla R_{z(k)}(\theta),\quad h\in(0,1),t\in[0,T],\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z=(z_{0},\dots,z_{B-1})\in\mathcal{Z}^{B},

\displaystyle Z_{n}:=

\displaystyle(\bm{z}(0+Bn),\dots,\bm{z}((B-1)+Bn)),\quad n\in\mathbb{N},

\displaystyle f_{t}^{h}(\theta):=

\displaystyle F(h,t,\theta,Z_{\left\lfloor t/h\right\rfloor}),\quad h\in(0,1),t\in[0,T],\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then

|F(h,t,\theta,z)|\lesssim 1+|\theta|,\text{ uniformly over }h\in(0,1),t\in[0,T],\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},z\in\mathcal{Z}^{B},

and
f_{t}^{h}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}(\mathbb{R}^{d},L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{d}))
uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
and
h\in(0,1)
.
Further, by defining

\bar{f}_{t}(\theta):=-u_{t}\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta),\quad\Sigma_{t}(\theta):=\frac{u_{t}^{2}}{B}\mathbb{E}[(\nabla R_{\bm{z}(0)}(\theta)-\nabla\mathcal{R}(\theta))^{\otimes 2}],

for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\in[0,T]
, we have
\bar{f}\in\operatorname{Lip}^{l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d}),\Sigma\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, and

\bar{f}_{t}(\theta)=\mathbb{E}[f_{t}^{h}(\theta)],\quad\Sigma_{t}(\theta)=\operatorname{Cov}f_{t}^{h}(\theta),

for all
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},h\in(0,1)
and
t\in[0,T]
.

Proof 7.2
.

From
